Changes are persisted to a JSON\nsettings file and applied globally across the application.\n\nFeatures:\n- Color pickers for background and foreground elements (code, metadata, output, workspace).\n- Font selectors for code, metadata, and output sections.\n- Spin boxes for adjusting font sizes.\n- Reset-to-default functionality for restoring original settings.\n- Tabbed layout for future extensibility (e.g., advanced settings).\n\nComponents:\n- QTabWidget: Contains \"Appearance\" and \"Advanced\" tabs.\n- QVBoxLayout: Main layout with color previews and font controls.\n- QPushButton: Reset and Close actions.\n\nMethods:\n- select_color(key): Opens QColorDialog and updates preview + settings.\n- select_font(target): Opens QFontDialog and updates font preview + settings.\n- update_font_preview(target): Refreshes font preview label and saves size.\n- reset_to_defaults(): Restores all settings to DEFAULT_SETTINGS.\n- save_settings(): Writes current settings to setting.json.\n- load_settings(): Loads settings from file or creates default if missing.\n\nUsage:\nTypically invoked from the main menu or toolbar to personalize the IDE's look and feel.\nAll changes are immediately saved and reflected in editor components.\n[15 Aug 26 | 10:24:52]:\nA configuration panel for customizing appearance and font settings in Uranus IDE.\nThis class provides a tabbed interface for modifying UI colors, font families, and font sizes\nused across code editors, markdown cells, and output viewers. It supports\ninteractive exploration of structured types (e.g., list, dict, set) and file-like\nobjects via double-click, opening a detailed view of the full value.\n\nFeatures:\n---------\n- Displays object metadata in a QTableWidget:\n- Object name\n- Type (with color-coded background)\n- Size in bytes\n- Summarized value\n- Supports full value inspection via double-click on the \"Value\" column\n- Automatically detects structured types and file-like objects\n- Handles JSON serialization and fallback to reprlib for complex objects\n- Integrates with IPython shell or external namespace extractors\n- Designed to be extensible for future type-specific viewers (e.g., tree view, dataframe preview)\n\nAttributes:\n-----------\ntable : QTableWidget\nThe main table displaying object metadata.\ndata : List[Dict[str, Any]]\nInternal storage of full object metadata, including real values.\nknown_dtypes : Set[str]\nOptional filter for allowed types, used during namespace extraction.\n\nMethods:\n--------\nadd_objects(objects: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> None\nPopulates the table with a list of object metadata dictionaries.\nEach dictionary must contain 'name', 'type', 'size', and 'value'.\n\nshow_full_value(item: QTableWidgetItem) -> None\nOpens a dialog showing the full value of the selected object.\nSupports structured types and file-like objects.\n\nis_structured_type(value: Any) -> bool\nReturns True if the value is a list, dict, set, tuple, or frozenset.\n\nsummarize_value(value: Any, max_len: int = 80) -> str\nReturns a short string summary of the value for display in the table.\n\ninspect_all_user_attributes(shell: InteractiveShell) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]\nExtracts user-defined objects from an IPython shell, filtering out\nbuilt-ins and system objects.               "code": "1|def highlightBlock(self, text):\n2|    \n3|    # Reset block state for multi-line string handling\n4|    self.setCurrentBlockState(0)\n5|    \n6|    # Get absolute positions of this block in the document\n7|    block_start = self.currentBlock().position()\n8|    block_end = block_start + len(text)\n9|    \n10|    # Update triple-quote cache if needed\n11|    self._update_triple_quotes_if_needed()\n12|    \n13|    # ============================================================\n14|    # STEP 1: Initialize tracking variables\n15|    # ============================================================\n16|    \n17|    # Track which characters are already formatted (to prevent overlap)\n18|    formatted_mask = [False] * len(text)\n19|    \n20|    # Track if we're inside a single-line string\n21|    in_string = False\n22|    string_char = None\n23|    \n24|    # ============================================================\n25|    # STEP 2: Process triple-quoted strings (HIGHEST PRIORITY)\n26|    # ============================================================\n27|    \n28|    for start_offset, end_offset in self.triple_quote_ranges:\n29|        # Check if this block intersects with a triple-quoted block\n30|        if start_offset <= block_end and end_offset >= block_start:\n31|            # Calculate local start and end positions within this block\n32|            start = max(start_offset, block_start) - block_start\n33|            end = min(end_offset, block_end) - block_start\n34|            \n35|            # Safety check\n36|            if start < len(text) and end <= len(text):\n37|                # Format as string\n38|                self.setFormat(start, end - start, self.string_format)\n39|                \n40|                # Mark these positions as \"already formatted\"\n41|                for i in range(start, end):\n42|                    if i < len(text):\n43|                        formatted_mask[i] = True\n44|    \n45|    # ============================================================\n46|    # STEP 3: Process single-line strings (\", ')\n47|    # ============================================================\n48|    # This uses a simple state machine to track string boundaries\n49|    # It respects escape sequences (\\) and ignores quotes inside strings\n50|    \n51|    i = 0\n52|    while i < len(text):\n53|        # Skip if already formatted (inside triple-quote)\n54|        if formatted_mask[i]:\n55|            i += 1\n56|            continue\n57|        \n58|        char = text[i]\n59|        \n60|        # Check if this is a quote character\n61|        if char in ('\"', \"'\"):\n62|            # Find the matching closing quote\n63|            j = i + 1\n64|            found_match = False\n65|            \n66|            while j < len(text):\n67|                # Check if this is the closing quote (not escaped)\n68|                if text[j] == char and text[j-1] != '\\\\':\n69|                    # Found a match!
